# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-11-05_TotalExtraction_75925096.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
November 12, 2024  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook (arriving at 6:03 
p.m.), Ken Esquibel (via Zoom), Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne (via Zoom), 
Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White. Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk. The pledge of 
allegiance was recited. 
 
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received 
by Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Cook, items #29(e), 29(f) and 29(g) were removed from the Consent 
Agenda. Mr. Segrave moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Dr. Aldrich. Motion carried. 
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on October 28, 2024. Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402 exist for about 640 acres of land situate in 
portions of Sections 4 and 5, T13N, R67W, 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (also known as 
North Range Business Park; located northwest of the I-80 and Roundtop Road Interchange). 
Comment was made by the following member of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich. There being no 
further comment or input and the purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed 
the public hearing. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402 exist for the City-initiated annexation of 38 
parcels of land totaling 26.67 acres comprised of various tracts of land situate in northeast 
Cheyenne generally located north of Bevans Street, west of College Drive and south of Dell Range 
Boulevard. Comment was made by the following member of the governing body Dr. Aldrich. 
There being no further comment or input and the purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor 
Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402 exist for the City-initiated annexation of 26 
parcels of land totaling 38.37 acres comprised of various tracts of land situate in northeast 
Cheyenne generally located east of Ridge Road, west of Whitney Road and along Dell Range 
Boulevard. Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, 

Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson and Mr. Laybourn. There being no further comment or input and the 
purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for disposal of real property and land exchange 
between the City of Cheyenne and Black Hills Energy (properties generally located at the 800 
block of W. 18th Street and the 1300 block of W. 24th Street). Comment was made by the 
following member of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich. There being no further comment or input 
and the purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne adding the Urban Use Overlay District to land located north of East 21st Street and east 
of and adjacent to Warren Avenue. Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Roybal advised 
there was no recommendation from the committee. Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Segrave voting “no”. Public comment 
was made by: Elizabeth Valverde-Bartlett.  Comments were made by the following members of 
the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Segrave. (#4591) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Authorizing the Contractor Licensing Board to license 
and otherwise regulate portable fire extinguisher, hose station equipment and hood system cleaning 
and maintenance professionals, making conforming amendments, and repealing obsolete 
provisions of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. Reporting for Finance 
Committee, Dr. Aldrich moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White. 
Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook 
and Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Comment was made by the following member of the governing 
body: Mr. Cook. (#4592) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, tracts of 
land situate in portions of Sections 4 and 5, T13N, R67W, 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming 
(also known as ‘North Range Business Park’, located northwest of the I-80 and Roundtop Road 
interchange) – referred to Public Services Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal)    
 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classification of LI Light Industrial and BP Business Park for 
tracts of land being annexed to the City situate in portions of Sections 4 and 5, T13N, R67W, 6th 
P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (also known as ‘North Range Business Park’, located northwest 
of the I-80 and Roundtop Road interchange) – referred to Public Services Committee.  (Sponsor – 
Mr. Roybal)    
 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from AG Agricultural and HI Heavy Industrial to AG 
Agricultural for land located south of Campstool Rd and north of Interstate 80 – referred to Public 
Services Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal)    
 
 

 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, various 
tracts of land completely surrounded by current City limits situate in northeast Cheyenne generally 
located north of Bevans Street, west of College Drive and south of Dell Range Boulevard – referred 
to Public Services Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classification of MR - Medium-Density Residential, HR - High 
Density Residential, and CB - Community Business for land annexed to the City of Cheyenne 
generally located north of Bevans Street, west of College Drive, and south of Dell Range 
Boulevard – referred to Public Services Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, various 
tracts of land completely surrounded by current City limits situate in northeast Cheyenne generally 
located east of Ridge Road, west of Whitney Road and along Dell Range Boulevard – referred to 
Public Services Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classification of AR - Agricultural Residential, LR Low-Density 
Residential, MR - Medium-Density Residential, and MUR Mixed-Use Residential for land 
annexed to the City of Cheyenne generally located east of Ridge Road, west of Whitney Road and 
along Dell Range Boulevard – referred to Public Services Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from P Public and MUR Mixed-Use Residential to 
MUB Mixed-Use Business for land located northeast of the intersection of Morrie Avenue and 
East Lincolnway – referred to Public Services Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Section 8.60.080, Non-Compliant Vehicles 
as a Nuisance, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, declaring oversized 
vehicles as a nuisance, providing for their regulation, clarifying provisions and making conforming 
amendments – referred to Committee of the Whole.  (Sponsors – Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Johnson) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Creating Chapter 13.32, Stormwater Runoff 
Management System, of Title 13, Public Services, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, establishing the Stormwater Runoff Management System, imposing a user fee for 
necessary funding and repealing obsolete provisions – referred to Committee of the Whole.  
(Sponsor – Mr. Johnson) 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Specifying the monthly Stormwater Runoff 
Management System User Fee in accordance with Section 13.32.050 of the Municipal Code of the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Committee of the Whole.  (Sponsor – Mr. Johnson) 
 
[CA]  
RESOLUTION – Acknowledging receipt of the Rotary Park Master Plan Report. Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6436) 
 

[CA]  
RESOLUTION – Expressing the intent of the Governing Body, repealing and replacing 
Resolution No. 6127 governing the creation and administration of the Innovation and Entrepreneur 
Advisory Council. Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6437) 
 
  
RESOLUTION – Acknowledging receipt of a petition by the City of Cheyenne Planning 
Commission to add the UU Urban Use Overlay to an area zoned MUR Mixed-Use Residential 
Emphasis with frontage on the east side of Warren Avenue between 21st and 22nd Streets, and 
directing staff to initiate a zoning map amendment pursuant to the petition. Mr. Roybal advised 
there was no recommendation from Public Services Committee. Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, 
seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion failed due to a tie vote. Voting “yes” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, 
Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Roybal.  Voting “no” – Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Mr. Laybourn, 
Mr. Segrave and Mr. White. Comments were made by the following members of the governing 
body: Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Segrave. 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Directing City staff to research and develop a plan to initiate the process 
to pave the road on Birch Place between Charles Street and Cheyenne Street and install curb, gutter 
and sidewalks. Mr. Roybal advised there was no recommendation from Public Services 
Committee. Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Johnson. Motion failed. Voting “no” – 
all members of the governing body. Comments were made by the following members of the 
governing body: Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Johnson. 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Directing City staff to research and develop a plan to initiate the process 
to pave the road on Ridge Road between Hillcrest Road and Stevens Drive and install curb, gutter 
and sidewalks. Mr. Roybal advised there was no recommendation from Public Services 
Committee. Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Johnson. Motion failed. Voting “no” – 
all members of the governing body. Comments were made by the following members of the 
governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins and Mr. Johnson. 
 
[CA]  
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City Treasurer to discharge uncollectible debts pursuant 
to Wyoming Statute § 16-4-502 – referred to Finance Committee.  (Sponsor – Dr. Aldrich) 
 
[CA]  
RESOLUTION – Certifying Planning Commission action amending the Future Land Use 
maps of PlanCheyenne changing the land use category from Open Space and Parks to Mixed-Use 
Commercial for property generally located northeast of the intersection of Morrie Avenue and East 
Lincolnway – referred to Public Services Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA]  
RESOLUTION – Directing City staff to evaluate and determine an effective drainage plan 
for the intersection of Bevans Street and Wills Road and the adjacent areas – referred to Public

[CA]  
RESOLUTION – By the Governing Body designating a Paid On-Street Parking District 
for Downtown Cheyenne in accordance with Section 10.60.015 of the Municipal Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. Johnson) 
  
 

  
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Metropolitan Planning 
Organization (MPO), and Kimley-Horn and Associates, Inc., for an update of the MPO Long-
Range Metropolitan Transportation Plan (MTP) (MPO Fund). Dr. Aldrich moved to approve in an 
amount not to exceed $199,841.82, seconded by Mr. White. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all

[CA]  
Grant of Utility Easement between the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Board of Public 
Utilities on city owned property situated in the S½ of Section 35, Township 14 North, Range 66

[CA]

[CA]  
Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and the World Resources

Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Safe Harbor to provide a safe and secure

Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and The Cheyenne Housing Authority to provide

Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mr.

Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Volunteers of America Northern Rockies,

Aldrich moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $136,875.00, seconded by Mr. White. 
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. Public comment was made 
by: Heidi McNeil, Volunteers of America Northern Rockies and Brandon Swain.  Comments were

[CA]  
Telecommunications Right-of-Way License and Use Agreement between the City of 
Cheyenne and Viaero Fiber Networks, LLC for the installation and operation of facilities. 
(Revenue to City). Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#8086) 
 

  
Agreement for the purchase, sale and exchange of lands between the City of Cheyenne and 
Black Hills Energy for exchange of real property, 0.77 acres, from the City to Black Hills Energy 
and 0.85 acres, from Black Hills Energy to the City, both having appraised values of $275,000.00 
and $445,000.00, respectively (properties generally located at 108 W. 18th Street and the 1300 
block of W. 24th Street) (2017 Specific Purpose Option Tax).  Dr. Aldrich moved to approve in 
an amount not to exceed $170,000.00, seconded by Mr. White. Dr. Aldrich moved to amend by 
substitute memo dated November 4, 2024, seconded by Mr. White. Motion to amend by substitute 
memo carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. Main motion as amended by 
substitute memo carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#8087) 
 
 
Utility Easement Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Light, Fuel 
and Power Company, doing business as Black Hills Energy, to maintain, operate, remove, and 
replace utility infrastructure (Revenue to City).  Dr. Aldrich advised the item was heard under

provisions of Title 2 of City Code had been met.  Dr. Aldrich moved to approve in the amount of 
$10.00, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. (#8088) 
 
[CA] 
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Wyoming Office of Homeland Security 
to support expenses and equipment costs for the Cheyenne Fire Rescue RERT7 Team through the

to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Public Facility and Circulation Easement Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and 
Magic City Enterprises Inc. regarding access to the properties between 1780 and 1800 Westland

[CA] 
Contract Modification #2 to Contract #7480 between the City of Cheyenne and Veolia ES 
Technical Solutions for Hazardous Waste Disposal Services through January 31, 2026 (Solid 
Waste Fund). Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $200,000.00). 
(#7480) 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #2 to Contract #7462 between the City of Cheyenne and Onsite 
Electronics Recycling, LLC, d/b/a I.T. Refresh for Electronics Recycling Services through January 
31, 2026 (Solid Waste Fund). Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed 
$200,000.00). (#7462) 
 
 
Contract Modification #1 Contract #8057 between the City of Cheyenne and Orion 
Environmental In., for additional abatement work for the Cheyenne Railcar Project, Bid Package 
No. 1, Asbestos and Lead Paint Abatement Project (2021 Specific Purpose Option Tax Fund, 
General Fund Reserves & EDA Grant 0579-06167). Dr. Aldrich moved to approve in an amount 
not to exceed $91,846.00, seconded by Mr. Johnson. Dr. Aldrich moved to amend by substitute 
dated October 30, 2024, seconded by Mr. White. Motion to amend by substitute carried. Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. Main motion, as amended by substitute carried. Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Laybourn 
voting “no”. Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich 
and Mr. Laybourn. (#8057)  

 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-5-25 for mobile clam shell style laptops and docking stations for 
the IT Division.  (2019-2022 & 2023-2026 Optional 1% Sales Tax Fund). Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $78,997.44). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-4-25 for (3) Fire Apparatus for the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne 
Fire Rescue Department (Future Ballot Specific Purpose Option Tax Fund) – referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Consideration to Purchase between the City of Cheyenne and Flock Group Inc., d/b/a Flock 
Safety, for the purchase of Flock Safety License Plate Readers and Flock OS Software for the City 
of Cheyenne Police Department (ARPA Grant) – referred to Finance Committee.   
 
 
Governing body comment was made by Mr. Johnson on the Voucher Report as presented. 
       
 
Public comment was made by the following under Other Business: Brian Deurloo, Frog 
Creek Partners, Alan Sheldon and Brandon Swain.  
 
The following members of the governing body made comments under Other Business: Dr. 
Aldrich and Mr. Laybourn.  
 
Mr. Roybal moved to meet in executive session pursuant to Wyoming Statute §16-4- 
405(a)(vii), seconded by Dr. Aldrich. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. Mayor Collins announced members would meet in executive session, not open to the public 
and stated there would be no action taken by the governing body as a result of the executive 
session, and that the regular City Council meeting would officially conclude at the close of the 
executive session. The following staff members were in attendance: Kris Jones, City Clerk; Amber 
Ash, Mayor’s Chief of Staff; Stefanie Boster, City Attorney; Tom Cobb, City Engineer; Craig 
LaVoy, Public Works Deputy Director; Jean Anderson-Shrednik, Greenway and Parks Planner; 
Seth Lloyd, Planning & Development and Charles Bloom, Planning & Development Director. 
[Clerk’s note: Mr. Esquibel was not present during executive session].     
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:04 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kris Jones, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
City Clerk

MEMO
To:
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council
From:
Seth Lloyd, AICP
Subject:
PUDC-24-89 | North Range Business Park
Annexation
Date:
November 12, 2024
Background: 
The item for consideration is an Annexation.  This is approved by Ordinance.   
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows:
Introduction and Referral 
11/12/2024
Public Services Committee 
11/19/2024
Second reading 
11/25/2024
Public Services Committee 
12/03/2024
Third and final reading 
12/09/2024
Attachments:
1.
Ordinance
2.
Staff Report
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE ANNEXING TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING, TRACTS OF LAND SITUATE IN PORTIONS OF SECTIONS 
4 AND 5, T13N, R67W, 6TH P.M., LARAMIE COUNTY, WYOMING (ALSO 
KNOWN 
AS 
'NORTH 
RANGE 
BUSINESS 
PARK', 
LOCATED 
NORTHWEST OF THE I-80 AND ROUNDTOP ROAD INTERCHANGE)." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1. That the City of Cheyenne hereby finds as follows:
(a) That the City of Cheyenne has initiated this annexation in compliance with Wyo.
Stat. § 15-1-404, and has adopted a Resolution certifying that the area to be annexed 
complies with the requirements of Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-402 (said resolution being 
adopted on July 22, 2024, as Resolution #6418); 
 
(b) That an accurate legal description of said land is as follows:  
 
Various tracts of land situate in northwest Cheyenne more particularly described as 
follows:  
 
Lot 3, Block 1, North Range Business Park; Lots 1-2, Block 6, North Range 
Business Park, 3rd Filing; Lots 1-2, Block 8, North Range Business Park, 3rd 
Filing; Lots 1-5, Block 9, North Range Business Park, 3rd Filing; Lots 1-6, 
Block 10, North Range Business Park, 3rd Filing; Lots 1-2, Block 11, North 
Range Business Park, 3rd Filing; Lot 2, Block 12, North Range Business Park, 
4th Filing; Lot 3, Block 1, North Range Business Park, 5th Filing; Lots 1-2, 
Block 13, North Range Business Park, 6th Filing; Lots 2-4, Block 15, North 
Range Business Park, 8th Filing; Lot 1, Block 17, North Range Business Park, 
10th Filing; Lot 1, Block 16, North Range Business Park, 11th Filing; Lot1, 
Block 18, North Range Business Park, 12th Filing; Lots 1-3, Block 1, North 
Range Business Park, 13th Filing; Lot 1, Block 1, North Range Substation; Lot 
1, Block 1, BHE Ready Subdivision; and the rights of way for Diamond Creek 
Drive, Veta Drive, Logistics Drive, McKinney Drive, and Horizon Drive as 
platted with North Range Business Park, 3rd Filing (noting that “Diamond 
Creek Drive” as shown on the plat was renamed “Bruns Way” on April 7, 2020, 
by action of the Board of County Commissioners), Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
(c) That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; 
(d) That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; 
(e) That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 

basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and other 
services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, be 
furnished;
(f) That the area is considered contiguous to the City of Cheyenne according to the 
provisions of Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-402 (b) and is currently zoned LI Light Industrial, 
PUD (Resolution #091006-32, recorded Book 2159, Page 1128), and A-2 -
Agricultural by the Laramie County Commissioners; and
(g) That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and its
governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the entire 
annexed area. 
Section 2. That all of the real property described in Section 1(b) of this Ordinance is hereby 
annexed to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
Section 3.  That pursuant to Wyoming Statute § 15-1-405, the Governing Body held a 
public hearing on November 12, 2024, on the proposed and annexation and found that the 
annexation complied Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-402.   
Section 4. That the map of the area to be annexed is shown in Exhibit A, which is attached 
hereto and incorporated herein, satisfies the requirements of Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-406 and shall be 
recorded with the county clerk in conjunction with this ordinance. 
Section 5. That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming, 
and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to the official City Map of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared illegal 
or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining 
phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

CITY OF CHEYENNE GOVERNING BODY

STAFF REPORT
FILE:
PUDC-24-89: North Range Business Park - Annexation
REQUEST:
A City-initiated annexation of 640 acres of land
LOCATION:
Northwest of the intersection of Roundtop Road and I-80
APPLICANT:
City of Cheyenne
OWNERS:
Microsoft Corporation, Cheyenne Light Fuel and Power Co, Walmart Stores 
East Ltd Ptnsp, Cheyenne Leads, Tri State Generation and Transmission Assoc 
Inc, Mine One Wyoming Data Center LLC, North Range Business Park Comm 
Owners Assoc, UMC Prop LLC, Westcom Properties LLC, University Corp For 
Atmospheric Research, Advanced Communications Technology Inc, YZY Capital 
Holdings LLC
PURPOSE:
Annexation of North Range Business Park
PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, AICP, Senior Planner
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

PUDC-24-89
November 12, 2024
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This project annexes North Range Business Park. The business park was established with various Outside 
User Agreements to utilize BOPU water and sewer. These agreements included a stipulation that the land 
would be annexed once eligible according to state statute. With an annexation south of I-80 occurring earlier 
in 2024, the business park became eligible for annexation and the City initiated annexation in accordance 
with the outside user agreements. Any City-intiated annexation requires a resolution to be passed prior to 
the public hearing and passage of the Ordinance. The resolution for this project was adopted on July 22, 
2024, as Resolution #6418. The annexed area will be assigned City zoning based on the underlying County 
zones.  
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. The annexation will be accompanied by assigned City zones of 
BP Business Park, LI Light Industrial, and AG Agricultural.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make the following findings required in 
WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)]

PUDC-24-89
November 12, 2024
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the future 
occupants. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to City Fire and Police and 
sanitation services. The property, if occupied, will also be required to be connected to City water 
and sanitary sewer instead of having wells and septic systems.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
 
This property, once developed, will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part 
of the city. The property is adjacent to the City to the south. The area already has urban services 
in the form of water and sewer provision. The contiguity and existing City services make the 
parcel a natural geographic part of the City. The development of this property will benefit those 
who live within the City and will be a part of the social and economic fabric that is Cheyenne.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. City water and sewer is already being provided to the subject 
property. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation is not contiguous, but is adjacent across the I-80 right-of-way. 
Highway rights-of-way do not adversely affect contiguity per Wyoming State Statute  
15-1-402 (b). If the I-80 right-of-way is not counted, the property would be 19% contiguous to 
the south. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 

PUDC-24-89
November 12, 2024
utilities within the territory and by first class mail to any persons owning property that is 
adjacent to or within three hundred (300) feet of the territory proposed to be annexed, 
regardless of whether the property is inside or outside the corporate limits of the annexing 
city or town and regardless of whether the city or town is exercising authority under  
W.S. 15-3-202(b)(ii), a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on October 10, 2024. 
-class letters the same day. The public hearing for this 
annexation is scheduled for November 12, 2024. Twenty (20) business days 
calendar) prior to the announced public hearing is October 14, 2024. The earliest confirmation 
of delivery was dated October 11, 2024. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with mixed-use employment uses. These areas are generally expected to develop 
with full access to urban services  such areas are best served by being within City limits. The area is 
within the Urban Service Boundary. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits [with 
the exception allowed by Wyoming State Statute 15-1-402 (b)], and most services typically available to 
urban areas are available to be extended to this property.  
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map

ESTABLISHING 
THE 
ZONING 
CLASSIFICATION OF LI LIGHT INDUSTRIAL AND BP BUSINESS 
PARK FOR TRACTS OF LAND BEING ANNEXED TO THE CITY 
SITUATE IN PORTIONS OF SECTIONS 4 AND 5, T13N, R67W, 6TH P.M., 
LARAMIE COUNTY, WYOMING (ALSO KNOWN AS 'NORTH RANGE 
BUSINESS PARK', LOCATED NORTHWEST OF THE I-80 AND 
ROUNDTOP ROAD INTERCHANGE)." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That the City of Cheyenne is initiating the annexation of property legally 
described as:  
 
Lot 3, Block 1, North Range Business Park; Lots 1-2, Block 6, North Range Business Park, 
3rd Filing; Lots 1-2, Block 8, North Range Business Park, 3rd Filing; Lots 1-5, Block 9, 
North Range Business Park, 3rd Filing; Lots 1-6, Block 10, North Range Business Park, 
3rd Filing; Lots 1-2, Block 11, North Range Business Park, 3rd Filing; Lot 2, Block 12, 
North Range Business Park, 4th Filing; Lot 3, Block 1, North Range Business Park, 5th 
Filing; Lots 1-2, Block 13, North Range Business Park, 6th Filing; Lots 2-4, Block 15, 
North Range Business Park, 8th Filing; Lot 1, Block 17, North Range Business Park, 10th 
Filing; Lot 1, Block 16, North Range Business Park, 11th Filing; Lot1, Block 18, North 
Range Business Park, 12th Filing; Lots 1-3, Block 1, North Range Business Park, 13th 
Filing; Lot 1, Block 1, North Range Substation; Lot 1, Block 1, BHE Ready Subdivision; 
and the rights of way for Diamond Creek Drive, Veta Drive, Logistics Drive, McKinney 
Drive, and Horizon Drive as platted with North Range Business Park, 3rd Filing (noting 
that “Diamond Creek Drive” as shown on the plat was renamed “Bruns Way” on April 7, 
2020, by action of the Board of County Commissioners), Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
 
Section 2.  That Section 2.2.8 of the UDC provides standards and procedures for zoning 
districts established on land or property currently without a City zone assignment including land 
being annexed to the city.  
 
 
Section 3.  That on November 4, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission 
conducted public hearings, reviewed the proposed zoning assignment that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zoning assignment.  
 
 
Section 4.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning 
assignment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.8.d of 
the UDC. 
 
 
Section 5.  That in accordance with Section 2.2.8, Assigned Zoning, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the zoning 
6:02 pm, Nov 04 2024

classifications of the real property as described in Section 1 is hereby established as LI Light 
Industrial for lands currently zoned County LI Light Industrial and BP Business Park for lands 
currently zoned County PUD (Resolution #091006-32, recorded Book 2159, Page 1128). 
 
 
Section 6.   That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend 
the Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 8 below. 
 
 
Section 7.   Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 8.   That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication, and upon approval and publication of the affiliated annexation.

2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001

(Fax) 307-637-6366

PUDC-24-89 
 
 
November 4, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve the assigned zoning for land being annexed (as 
shown in the attached annexation map) located northwest of the intersection of Roundtop Road and  
I-80 to LI Light Industrial for land currently zoned County LI Light Industrial, and to BP Business Park for 
land zoned County PUD Planned Unit Development, noting that the project meets the review criteria for 
approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.2.8 Assigned Zoning 
 
BACKGROUND: 
The City of Cheyenne is initiating the annexation of the North Range Business Park. This business park has 
been using Cheyenne water and sewer service as an outside user. On typical outside user agreements 
(including the ones entered into here), annexation when eligible is a condition. Recent annexation south of 
this property has made the property eligible for annexation. Due to an oversight with the required notice, the 
County A-2 zoned parcel was overlooked. The parcel zoned A-2 Agricultural will return under a separate 
application to be zoned AG Agricultural within the City. 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The assigned zone is the most analogous City zone to the current zone when considering 
allowed uses. Zoning for standard County zones shall be assigned based on the policy 
document, while zones not listed on the policy document and terminated City zones shall be 
assigned a City zone on a case-by-case basis. (UDC 2.2.8.d.1) 
 
The underlying county 
zones are County LI Light 
Industrial, County PUD 
Planned Unit 
Development, and 
County A-2 Agricultural. 
According to the policy 
document, the most 
analogous city zone for 
the County LI zone is LI 
Light Industrial, and the 
most analogous city 
zone for the County A-2 
zone is AG Agricultural. 
The Assigned Zoning 
policy document is 
attached to the staff 
report as attachment 
number two.  
Current Zoning 

PUDC-24-89 
 
 
November 4, 2024 
The County PUD zone allows a wide variety of industrial, retail, and service uses. Transportation, 
recreation, and educational facilities are also allowed. The City BP Business Park zone is the City 
zone which provides for the widest variety of industrial, retail, and service uses. The MUE zone 
also allows most of these uses, but limits these uses to smaller-scale versions. The PUD 
applicable in the area being annexed is attached to the staff report as attachment number 
three. 
 
Establishment of the City zone matches the policy document and the PUD is assigned the most 
analogous City zone based on the allowed uses; therefore, the item complies with review 
criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, there were no comments.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.8.(d). 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the Assigned Zoning based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the Assigned Zoning until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or recommends conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for their recommendation. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the assigned zoning for land being annexed northwest of the intersection 
of Roundtop Road and I-80 to LI Light Industrial, and BP Business Park, noting that the project meets the 
review criteria for approval. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
 
1. Annexation Map 
2. Assigned Zoning Policy Document 
3. County PUD applicable in the annexation area and direct comparison to the BP zone

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM AG AGRICULTURAL AND HI HEAVY INDUSTRIAL TO AG 
AGRICULTURAL FOR LAND LOCATED SOUTH OF CAMPSTOOL RD 
AND NORTH OF INTERSTATE 80." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”). 
 
 
Section 2. That on November 4, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission 
conducted a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zoning map amendment that is the subject of 
this ordinance, and recommended approval of said zoning map amendment. 
 
 
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning map 
amendment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of 
the UDC. 
 
 
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as Lot 1, Block 1 Cheyenne Business Parkway Thirteenth Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
is hereby changed to AG Agricultural. 
 
 
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 7 below. 
 
 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
PUDC-24-194: Lot 1, Block 1 Cheyenne Business Parkway Thirteenth 
Filing, – Zoning Map Amendment 
REQUEST: 
A request to rezone approximately 38.04 acres of land from a mix of AG 
Agricultural and HI Heavy Industrial to AG Agricultural. 
LOCATION: 
North of I-80, west of the I-80 and Campstool Road interchange 
APPLICANT: 
Brad Emmons, AVI PC 
AGENT: 
  AVI PC 
OWNER: 
  Cheyenne LEADS 
ZONING: 
AG Agricultural and HI Heavy Industrial 
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Open Space and Parks and Industrial 
PURPOSE: 
To modify the zoning in accordance with a lot line adjustment.

Brandon T. Gilchrist , Planner I 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-24-194 
 
 
November 4, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lot1 Block 1 Cheyenne Business Parkway, 13th Filing, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming to AG Agricultural as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets 
the review criteria for approval.  
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
• 
UDC Article 5 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The parcel is part of a lot line adjustment. The parent parcels are zoned HI Heavy Industrial on the east and 
AG Agricultural on the west. The AG parcel is essentially obtaining part of the HI parcel. As it is not best 
practice to have properties with multiple zones, the area added to the AG parcel should also be zoned AG.   
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
According to the Future Land Use Map, the majority of the site is planned for Open Space, Parks. 
This designation is intended to include natural/cultural resource areas, and is expected to 
include conservation purposes.  This property has a creek and a floodplain.. The proposed 
zoning change from a mix of AG Agricultural and HI Heavy Industrial to entirely AG Agricultural 
aligns with the goals of Plan Cheyenne. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1.

PUDC-24-194 
 
 
November 4, 2024 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
The neighborhood is currently zoned for Agricultural and Heavy Industrial uses. However, 
according to the Future Land Use Designation, the site in question is designated as Open Space, 
while the surrounding areas are planned for Industrial Districts, Open Space, and Mixed-Use 
Employment Emphasis. The current zoning of a majority of the parcel is AG Agricultural, and it is 
not best practice to have parcels with more than one zone. Therefore, changing the zoning of 
the site from a mix of AG Agricultural and HI Heavy Industrial to entirely AG aligns with the 
Future Land Use Designation and complies with review criteria 2. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
 
The proposed zoning change is suitable because it allows the entire site to be unified under a 
single zoning district. Since the section which is changing from HI Heavy Industrial is within a 
floodplain, development in that area is not permitted. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
Both the AG Agricultural and the HI Heavy Industrial zones do not have maximum block sizes, 
and therefore is unparsed by this criterion.   
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The AG Agricultural zoning is designed for agricultural uses and the preservation of lands in their 
natural state, with minimum investments in infrastructure. This zoning change will initially 
require minimal public infrastructure, adhering to rural-level standards. The AG District is 
suitable for areas where it is important to protect significant natural lands or open spaces, 
aligning with the Agriculture/Rural or Rural Residential categories in the Cheyenne 
Comprehensive Plan. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 

PUDC-24-194 
 
 
November 4, 2024 
 
The proposed zoning change is suitable because it allows the entire site to be unified under a 
single zoning district. Since the section currently zoned HI Heavy Industrial is within a floodplain, 
development in that area is not permitted. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no review comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d). The AG zone is 
appropriate based on the future land use map, surrounding zoning, and surrounding development 
patterns. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant.  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 

PUDC-24-194 
 
 
November 4, 2024 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from AG and HI to AG. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map
